# runs-recon report — M001

Headline correction before anything else: the tip of the finished test suite is **`run/flightcrew-characterization-2` at `4fd81d8`**, not `flightcrew-characterization`. The branch `flightcrew-characterization` sits at `68681c0` (2026-09-17), which is only a dev-workspace commit on top of `56ca431`, the run-2 *base*. None of run 2's suite work is on it: `flightcrew-characterization:flightdeck/testbench/` has no `case-map.json`, no `suites/hooks-decisions/`, no `suites/templates-slots/`, no `suites/sub-commands/`. The suite is finished, unmerged, and lives on one branch only.

## 1. Anatomy of a launch

The contract is `run/flightcrew-characterization-2:flightdeck/launch/README.md`. Three kinds of entry live under `flightdeck/launch/`: `specs/<S>/` is the cross-run home of one spec; `<L>/` is one run of one spec, created by `fc launch new` and named `<spec.name>-<n>`; and `README.md` plus `RUNLOG.md` beside them. Active-launch resolution scans only subdirectories holding a `launch.json`, and exactly one launch may be active.

A complete launch folder as it actually ran is `run/flightcrew-characterization-2:flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout-2/`. In production order:

1. `launch.json` — created by `fc launch new <spec-path>`. State record. Schema at `flightdeck/flightcrew/schemas/launch.schema.json`, template at `flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/launch.template.json`.
2. `specs/<S>/spec.v1.json` and `tests-map.v1.json` — pinned copies written by `fc launch pin`. Pinning sets `lock_commit`, and `fc launch pin tests-map` replaces `paths.allowed` with the map's `allowed_paths`.
3. `kickoff.md` — rendered by `fc launch kickoff` from parts under `flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/` (`base.md` plus a shape file plus a task file). `launch.json.kickoff.version` records the `'+'`-joined `part@version` list, e.g. `base@3+shape-workflow@2+task-feature@2`.
4. `plan.json` and `plan.md` — `fc plan write` stores, `fc plan render` renders.
5. `returns/` — `fc return worker|critic|verifier|explorer` stores agent returns. Also holds `<unit>.prompt.md`, the sealed dispatch written by `fc worker render <unit>`.
6. `events.jsonl` and `hooks.log` — appended by the hooks and by `fc events append`.
7. `evidence/` (per-check `T<n>.json`, plus `boundary.json`, `locked.json`, `budget.json`, `summary.json`) written by `fc check` / `fc verify`; `evidence.html` rendered by `fc evidence`.
8. `review/pass-<n>.json` and `review/resolutions.json` — critic passes, stored by `fc return critic`; resolutions by `fc return critic --resolve`.
9. `notes.md` — `fc launch note`.
10. `report.md` — `fc report`, final at `fc launch end`.
11. A `RUNLOG.md` entry, inserted by `fc launch end` through `fc runlog stub`.

**The run-2 launch folder is deliberately not that shape.** `run/flightcrew-characterization-2:flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-characterization/` holds only `launch.json`, `evidence/defects.md`, `runs/run-2/{kickoff.md, plan.json, report.md}` and the `specs/` interview material. No `events.jsonl`, no `hooks.log`, no `evidence/T*.json`, no `returns/`, no `plan.md`. Its `launch.json` has no `status` field, `"current_run": null` and `"runs": []`. It is a stub, not an active launch. Run 2 was conducted with no runner and no hooks, so none of the artefacts a launch normally produces exist.

## 2. Anatomy of a run

**The fc CLI.** `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc` is a `/bin/sh` shim that execs `node bin/fc.mjs`; nothing installs it. `fc.mjs` parses two global flags (`--launch`, `--json`), resolves the launch root through `checks/lib/launch-lib.mjs`, and dispatches to `bin/cmd/<command>.mjs`. Exit codes are fixed: 0 success, 1 usage or environment error, 2 failed check or blocking decision. Nineteen commands, 35 subcommands as the usage block in `bin/fc.mjs` lists them, which matches the report's count:

- `launch new|activate|status|phase|gate|end|pin|kickoff|escalate|note|land` (11)
- `check`, `verify`, `boundary`, `locked`, `budget`, `evidence`, `report`, `validate`, `distribute`, `doctor`, `return` (11 single)
- `events append|usage|summary` (3), `runlog stub|show` (2), `plan write|render` (2), `worker render|merge|return` (3), `lint spec` (1), `critic render` (1), `verifier render` (1)

`validate`, `lint`, `distribute` and `doctor` are the only commands that act without resolving a launch.

**Phases and gates.** `launch.schema.json` fixes the phase enum in order: `targets, plan, contracts, implement, verify, review, report, ended`. `fc launch phase` accepts only the immediately next one. Status enum: `draft, active, accepted, accepted-with-reservations, abandoned, partial`. Three gates, each `{status: pending|approved|exited, at, note}`. From `bin/cmd/launch.mjs:628-630`: G1 approve moves `plan` to `contracts`, G2 approve moves `contracts` to `implement`, G3 has no phase move and is the ending gate. `ceilings` carries `agents, implementers_concurrent, turns_per_agent, gate_iterations, stop_blocks, critic_passes, minutes, tokens, expected_tokens`. `stop_blocks` is capped at 8 because Claude Code ends the turn after eight consecutive Stop-hook blocks.

**Hooks.** Six scripts under `flightdeck/flightcrew/hooks/`, wired by `hooks/settings.fragment.json`, merged into `.claude/settings.json` by hand or by `fc distribute --apply --target <dir>`. They run in place; nothing is copied. Bindings:

- `event-log.mjs` on SessionStart, SessionEnd, SubagentStart, SubagentStop, TaskCreated, TaskCompleted, PostToolUseFailure, PermissionDenied, PreCompact, PostCompact, Stop, WorktreeRemove. Appends to `events.jsonl`. Always exit 0.
- `lock-guard.mjs` on PreToolUse matcher `Edit|Write|NotebookEdit`. Denies a target in `paths.locked` in every phase but `targets`. Prints a `deny` decision on stdout, exits 0.
- `boundary-guard.mjs` on the same event and matcher. Denies a target outside `paths.allowed` in phases `contracts, implement, verify, review`. Exits 0.
- `structural-check.mjs` on PostToolUse matcher `Edit|Write`. Runs the `structural` map's command for the file extension through `/bin/sh -c`. Exit 2 on failure, holding the turn.
- `stop-gate.mjs` on Stop, `timeout: 600`. In phase `verify` runs the acceptance gate, in phase `contracts` the contracts gate. Red means exit 2 and a `stop_block` event. At `min(ceilings.stop_blocks, 8)` consecutive blocks it appends `stall` and `trigger` and hands the decision to a human.
- `session-end.mjs` on SessionEnd. Best-effort `fc evidence` and `fc report`.

The no-op rule is the load-bearing safety property: a hook is silent whenever it cannot be sure it speaks for a run. `WorktreeCreate` is never hooked, because a command hook there replaces worktree creation rather than observing it.

**A check versus a gate.** A check is one row of the tests map, `flightdeck/flightcrew/schemas/tests-map.schema.json`, required fields `id, status, kind, covers, command, baseline`. `kind` is one of `structural, behavioural, artefact, invariant, project-rule, statistical, judged`; `class` is `deterministic, property, statistical, judged`. `command` is run as `/bin/sh -c` from the launch root. `gate_only: true` means it runs under `fc check all` and the stop gate only. `T1` is always the acceptance check. A gate is a phase barrier: three scripts under `flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/gates/`, each exporting `run(context)` returning `{ran, reason?, checks: [{id, verdict, blocking, code, output}], extra}`. `acceptance-gate.mjs` gates verify, `contracts-gate.mjs` gates contracts and also reports the boundary as a non-check blocker, `structural-gate.mjs` runs the per-file structural command. A gate never decides what to do about a red check. It reports, and the caller decides.

**The run report** comes from `fc report` (`bin/cmd/report.mjs`), rendering eight fixed sections from `evidence/summary.json`, `review/pass-*.json`, `plan.json`, `events.jsonl` and `notes.md`, to the shape in `templates/report.template.md`. A section whose input is absent prints its placeholder rather than disappearing, and the report never states an acceptance verdict.

**Workflows.** Three dynamic workflow scripts at `flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/`, copied to `.claude/workflows/` by `fc distribute --apply`. A launch whose kickoff shape is `shape-workflow` hands a wave to one; `shape-workflow` is chosen when a wave holds more units than `implementers_concurrent`. Each opens with a literal `export const meta`.

- `fc-implement.js`, phases Pilots then Units. Dispatches implementers wave by wave, pilots first, the rest in chunks of `implementers_concurrent`. Returns `{workflow, launch, timestamp, dispatched, returns, halt, stopped_on}`.
- `fc-review.js`, phases Review, Fix, Re-verify. Fresh critic per pass, routes findings by kind, stops on a spec conflict. Returns `{workflow, launch, timestamp, passes, fixes, verifications, unrouted, observations, escalation, trigger}`.
- `fc-explore.js`, phase Explore. One read-only explorer per question. Returns `{workflow, timestamp, asked, answers, rejected}`.

A script only dispatches agents and returns payloads. It reads no file, runs no command, and `Date.now()`, `new Date()` and `Math.random()` are unavailable, so a timestamp arrives through `args`. Nothing a script returns is stored until the orchestrator stores it with `fc return`.

## 3. Run 2 result

Source `run/flightcrew-characterization-2:flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-characterization/runs/run-2/report.md`. Base `56ca431`, landing commit `4046d1e`. Built with custom workflows, implementer per unit in a worktree, verifier rerun per unit, up to two repair rounds. No flightcrew runner, no hooks, no launch. Every agent ran on opus.

58 of 61 checks exit 0 on `4046d1e`, rerun from a cleared cache. `node flightdeck/testbench/run-all.mjs` exits 0 with 33 suites ok and hygiene ok. The critic pass verdict was pass with no blocking finding. Six units: U0 contracts, U1 subcommands, U2 hooks and templates, U3 roles/workflows/distributed, U4 schemas/validators/gates, U5 proof. Only U2 was refuted, twice.

The three red checks, verbatim:

- **T44 sweep-templates-1**, check concern 1. `flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/constitution-fragment.md` has no `{{slot}}`, JSON key or version comment, so the alteration appends ` renamed` to its first heading and nothing reads that heading. Only a snapshot of the heading text could turn a case red, which the README forbids. `suites/_checks/fixtures/sweep-exempt.json` is locked and empty. Deciding between an exemption and a different alteration is the human's call. The attempt to fix it (`014e2ec`) was reverted at `3043f6c`.
- **T32 spec-copies**, check concern 2. Red at the run's base before any unit ran. The three `spec.v1.json` copies differ from the frozen spec at `eca2bba`, from the run-2 setup commits `cfc9b21` and `56ca431`. `check-lib.mjs` hard-codes `SPEC_COMMIT` `ee8c088` and `FREEZE_COMMIT` `eca2bba`.
- **T37 scope-untouched**, check concern 3. Red at the run's base before any unit ran. Since `ee8c088` the setup commits changed the four crew files `critic, explorer, implementer, verifier`, `templates/worker-dispatch.template.md`, the matching `.claude/agents/flightcrew/` copies and `.claude/settings.json`.

Both T32 and T37 are red because of the run's own setup, not because of anything a unit did.

The six pinned defects, verbatim from `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-characterization/evidence/defects.md`:

1. `crew · max-turns-on-every-role-except-the-four-named [defect]` — every role except orchestrator, spec-builder, spec-judge and spec-attacker carries an integer maxTurns, but explorer, verifier and critic carry none. Ref `flightdeck/manuals/orchestration/crew.md:40`.
2. `crew · new-roles-match-the-roster [defect]` — explorer, implementer, verifier and critic carry the roster turns 12, 25, 15 and 20, but explorer, verifier and critic carry no maxTurns and implementer carries 200. Ref `flightdeck/flightcrew/crew/README.md:16`.
3. `crew · every crew body carries the inputs line [defect]` — spec-judge and spec-attacker carry none. Ref `flightdeck/manuals/orchestration/crew.md:11`.
4. `crew · every crew body is at most 60 lines [defect]` — the spec-builder body is longer. Ref `flightdeck/manuals/orchestration/crew.md:51`.
5. `e2e · fc launch gate G3 approve records a gate decision on a launch that has ended [defect]` — an ended launch should refuse a gate decision; today it exits 0 and records it. Ref `flightdeck/manuals/launch/launch-anatomy.md:73`.
6. `validate-tests-map · flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/validators/validate-tests-map.mjs judges coverage against the --spec file although the pinned spec sits beside the map [defect]` — the header says the spec beside the map is read first and `--spec` is the fallback; today `--spec` wins. Ref `flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/validators/validate-tests-map.mjs:8`.

Four non-blocking critic observations are left open: F1 on defect text form, F2 that run-all charges only new `git status --porcelain` lines so a write to an already-modified file is unseen, F3 that the session-end case accepts either the files or a `hooks.log` line explaining their absence, F4 that the worker-dispatch case claims a manual source it does not have.

## 4. What the tip believes the system is

Read from `run/flightcrew-characterization-2`, the real tip. Eleven roles under `flightdeck/flightcrew/crew/`, split into two chains by `crew/README.md`. The spec chain is `spec-builder, spec-judge, spec-attacker`, all fable. The run chain is `test-builder` (opus, 40 turns), `planner` (fable, 30), `orchestrator` (inherit, conducts and holds no Write or Edit), `implementer` (opus, 25, worktree isolation, acceptEdits), `verifier` (sonnet, 15), `critic` (fable, 20). `explorer` (haiku, 12) serves both chains. Ten schemas: `launch, plan, spec, tests-map, check-result, event, critic-findings, explorer-return, verifier-verdict, worker-return`. Three workflow scripts, six hooks, seven validators, one linter, three gates, and 33 suites plus the locked `_checks/` directory.

Against `main:flightdeck/flightcrew/` this is a different system. `main` has no `bin/`, no `hooks/`, no `workflows/`, no `launch/` and no `testbench/`. Its crew names roles that no longer exist: `isolated-worker.md`, `reviewer.md`, `spec-interviewer.md`, `worker.md`, plus a `crew.json`. It has three schemas where the tip has ten, and its templates are `liftoff.template.yaml`, `worker.template.md` and three `spec/*.spec.template.md` files, all gone at the tip. The diff is 115 files, 12,816 insertions against 3,296 deletions. `main` is the pre-buildout state. Treat nothing in `main:flightdeck/flightcrew/` as current.

## 5. What the worktree-wf_* commits reveal

The naming tells you the build method. `worktree-wf_<id>-<n>` is a Claude Code subagent worktree branch, one per implementer dispatch, and the shared `wf_` id groups a wave. Wave 1 was `wf_2fdadce5-244`, four concurrent unit branches `-1` through `-4`. `wf_835932ae-12f-1` was the U5 proof unit. `wf_9641b63f-a15-1` carries nothing beyond the base, so it is an abandoned or empty dispatch.

Every wave-1 branch forks from the same two U0 contracts commits, `3b6ef54` "Set the suite contracts: covers convention, defect helper, per-suite hygiene, case map" and `b148c10` "Rewrite the testbench README as the contract for the characterization suite". That is the method: contracts first, alone, then fan out. Each wave-1 branch then adds exactly one commit, and the titles are all of the same form, "name every X":

- `-1` `96610c7` "Name every runner subcommand by a case: e2e naming steps and a sub-commands suite"
- `-2` `ec7147a` "Add hooks-decisions and templates-slots suites naming every hook and template", then the repair pair `35c5fef` and `014e2ec`, then the revert `3043f6c`
- `-4` `142a746` "Name every schema, validator and gate from a case that runs it"
- `flightcrew-characterization-2/roles-workflows-distributed` `fc50711` "Name every role file, workflow script and distributed copy in the crew and workflows suites; pin the crew defects"
- `-3` holds nothing beyond `b148c10`, so U0's own worktree produced no further commit

Only U2's branch shows a repair cycle, which matches the report's "refuted twice". The orchestrator merged each branch by name (`5287889`, `7e74ace`, `26386fc`, `845e6f5`), then the proof unit `bd636d3`, then wrote the report at `4fd81d8`. Note also `flightcrew-characterization-1/contracts` at `95a8cae`, the salvaged partial from the abandoned run 1, held for the human and never merged.

## 6. Unfinished intent

- **The suite is not merged.** `run/flightcrew-characterization-2` is ahead of `flightcrew-characterization` by the entire run, and the report calls for a pull request the human merges. That PR does not exist in the branch state.
- **No scenario set exists.** `flightdeck/testbench/README.md` final section: statistical and judged checks would read `fixtures/scenarios/<set>/`, no set exists, and six checks (T16 to T21) pass empty under spec D3.
- **No judge runner is built.** `flightdeck/launch/specs/flightcrew-v1/spec.v1.json:661`: "Statistical and judged checks are representable in the tests map but no judge runner is built; their commands must emit the ratio or verdict-sheet path themselves. Deferred to a later version."
- **The run-2 RUNLOG entry is missing.** The newest entry in `flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md` is the abandoned run 1. No `fc launch end` ran, so no entry was stubbed.
- **The reviewer's acts are outstanding.** Report, Not done: "The reviewer's three acts on a fresh checkout and the transcript reading are the human's."
- **`case-map.json` is empty** (`{}`) by design, but by contract must be revisited whenever an earlier case is absorbed under a new name.
- **`sweep-exempt.json` is locked and empty**, which is exactly what makes T44 unresolvable inside the run.
- **Ten tooling faults from run 1 are promoted, not fixed.** `RUNLOG.md:10` lists them. The most severe is (1) "the orchestrator has no tool that invokes a workflow, so the workflow shape is unusable". Also (2) `fc worker render` refuses a contracts unit in phase contracts; (4) only frontmatter `maxTurns` limits an agent and it can stop a unit with no return; (5) a stopped agent cannot be resumed; (7) `fc budget` counts human waits as run minutes; (10) `fc return` has no `--stdin` while the orchestrator holds no Write.
- **Spec conflicts carried to the next version.** `RUNLOG.md:23`: B39 versus the workflow runtime, B27 worker render, the I14 deny rule that would block the test-builder, the stall message on exit 0 that Claude Code does not display, and C3 one-line output versus validator lines.
- **Six unresolved observations F6 to F11** in the `flightcrew-buildout-2` entry, each a named defect with a file and line.
- **`specs/flightcrew-v1/design.md:307`**: "These readings bind the implementation where the spec's letter left two possibilities. Each is a candidate amendment for spec v2."
- **The three-launch roadmap is unbuilt.** `dev/workspace/plans/flightcrew-features.md` names ten features F1 to F10 and splits them into `flightcrew-core` (F1, F2, F3, F6, F8), `flightcrew-conduct` (F4, F7, F9, F10) and `flightcrew-verdicts` (F5). It renames the runner from `fc` to `flight` and replaces `RUNLOG.md` with `FLIGHTLOG.md`. None of it exists on any branch I read.
- **`dev/workspace/plans/prd.md` and `architectural.md` are unfilled templates**, every section a bracketed prompt.
- **Empty scaffolds**: `flightdeck/launch/runs.keep`, `flightcrew/orchestration.keep`, `flightcrew/bin/runners.keep`, `flightcrew/checks/checks.keep`, `flightcrew/crew/agents.keep`, `testbench/tests.keep`, `testbench/benches/rubrics/spec/experiments/.keep`. The last is named in `specs/flightcrew-v1/design.md:21` as "the committed home for future rubric-bench chains".

## 7. Claims about the Claude Code harness I relied on

Each is a claim the system makes about the harness, not something I verified against Claude Code itself. A verifier should check these against current harness behaviour.

1. Twelve hook events are bound in `hooks/settings.fragment.json`: SessionStart, SessionEnd, SubagentStart, SubagentStop, TaskCreated, TaskCompleted, PostToolUseFailure, PermissionDenied, PreCompact, PostCompact, WorktreeRemove, PreToolUse, PostToolUse, Stop.
2. A PreToolUse hook blocks by printing a JSON decision (`permissionDecision` deny or ask, `decision` block, `continue` false) on stdout while exiting 0; a PostToolUse or Stop hook holds the turn by exiting 2. Source `hooks/README.md`.
3. Where two PreToolUse hooks answer on the same matcher, Claude Code takes the strictest. Source `hooks/README.md`.
4. Claude Code ends the turn after eight consecutive Stop-hook blocks. Source `schemas/launch.schema.json`, `ceilings.stop_blocks` maximum 8.
5. A `WorktreeCreate` command hook replaces worktree creation rather than observing it, must print the directory it created, and any non-zero exit aborts creation. Source `hooks/README.md`.
6. `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` resolves hook scripts from wherever a session starts, worktrees included. Source `hooks/settings.fragment.json`.
7. Claude Code gives hooks a minimal PATH that often excludes a version-manager shim, so `node` may not resolve. Source `hooks/README.md` Troubleshooting.
8. Hooks in `.claude/settings.json` need workspace trust before they run, and stay silent until it is granted. Source `hooks/README.md` Installing.
9. `worktree.baseRef` must be `head` or worker subagents branch from the remote default branch. Source `hooks/README.md`.
10. A workflow script is invoked as `/<name>` with its `args` object as structured data, and the runtime makes `Date.now()`, `new Date()` and `Math.random()` unavailable. Source `workflows/README.md`.
11. The workflow runtime rejects a script that ends with `export default`. Source `RUNLOG.md:23`, listed as spec conflict B39 and disputed against the spec.
12. A subagent worktree branches from HEAD, so an uncommitted launch-folder file is invisible to the worker. Source `workflows/README.md`.
13. A stopped agent cannot be resumed. Source `RUNLOG.md:10` fault 5.
14. Only the frontmatter `maxTurns` actually limits an agent, of three turn numbers that exist. Source `RUNLOG.md:10` fault 4.
15. A stall message on exit 0 is not displayed by Claude Code. Source `RUNLOG.md:23`.
16. The permissions allowlist names `Agent(<role>)` and `Workflow(<name>)` permission forms. Source `hooks/settings.fragment.json`.
17. A `sandbox.filesystem.deny` list applies to Bash and its children, where `permissions.deny` on file tools does not. Source `hooks/README.md`, "The hole the guards do not cover".

Scratchpad copy of the run-2 tree, if another crew member needs a path: `/private/tmp/claude-501/run2tree/`. I stayed read-only, checked out nothing, and wrote nothing into the repo. Two Explore subagents I dispatched for the CLI and the intent sweep failed to hand back, so I did both passes directly. Standing by for mission-cutter.

<teammate-message teammate_id="runs-recon" color="blue" summary="Addendum: G3 unenforced, subcommand count, defaults">
Addendum to runs-recon M001: the two explorer hand-backs were new to me, and they add one correction and several concrete details. Cap respected, 12 items.

# runs-recon addendum — M001

The two reports corroborate my sections 1 to 6 with no contradictions on the run 2 result, the defects, the branch topology or the unfinished-intent list. What follows is only what they add or change. I re-verified items 1 and 3 against the source myself; the rest are the explorers' reads, marked as such.

## Correction

1. **The subcommand count of 35 is one convention among two, and I overstated the agreement.** I said my usage-block count "matches the report's 35". That is true only under the suite's own convention: `suites/_checks/lib/parts.mjs` derives the inventory by parsing the usage text with `subcommandsFromUsage`, and the usage writes `validate <kind> [path]` and `return <kind> <file>` as placeholders, so each counts as one part. Reading each command module's own `help` instead expands `validate` into seven (`spec, tests-map, plan, launch, kickoff, return, all`) and `return` into four (`worker, explorer, verifier, critic`), giving 36 named verbs. Neither number is wrong. The checks count parts, not verbs, so 35 is the number the suite enforces. Explorer a94cbb2 flagged that it could not reproduce 35 and reported 36; that disagreement is a naming convention, not a defect.

## Material addition

2. **G3 is recorded but not enforced, and this explains pinned defect 5.** I verified it: across `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/`, `G3` appears only three times, in the `GATES` array (`cmd/launch.mjs:43`), in the usage string (`cmd/launch.mjs:31`), and in a display filter (`cmd/plan.mjs:69`). No phase move, no blocker function, and no reference in `launch end` or `launch land`. G1 and G2 each have a blocker check and a phase transition; G3 has neither. That is precisely why the e2e suite could pin "fc launch gate G3 approve records a gate decision on a launch that has ended" as a defect. The gate is a human sign-off record with no code behind it, so nothing refuses it. Worth treating as a mission seed rather than a curiosity, because the README describes three gates as though they were alike.

3. **Built-in defaults, verified at `cmd/launch.mjs:48-62`.** A launch created by `fc launch new` seeds `acceptance: 'T1'` and ceilings `agents 12, implementers_concurrent 4, turns_per_agent 25, gate_iterations 3, stop_blocks 8, critic_passes 2`, with the template's own values merged over them. The `structural` default map is also built in: `.mjs` runs `node --check`, `.js` runs `node --experimental-default-type=module --check`, `.json` parses through `JSON.parse`, `.sh` runs `sh -n`. Run 1 spending its 25-turn budget was the default, not a choice.

## Detail the explorers add to my section 2 (their reads, unverified by me)

4. `fc worker merge <unit>` does more than merge. It merges the unit branch, re-runs that unit's checks on the merged tree, commits, then removes the worktree and deletes the branch.
5. `fc launch end` requires fresh clean evidence before it will record an accepted outcome, and re-renders both `evidence.html` and `report.md` as it freezes.
6. `fc launch land --commit <sha> [--pr <url>]` records `launch.json.landed` only once `evidence/summary.json` at that commit is clean.
7. `fc budget` exits 2 and appends a `trigger` event when a ceiling is exceeded, so a budget breach is an abandon trigger rather than a warning.
8. `fc critic render` is valid only in phase `review`, and `fc verifier render` only in phase `verify`.
9. Return storage paths are fixed by kind: worker to `returns/<unit>.json`, explorer to `returns/explore-<id>.json`, verifier to `returns/verify-<n>.json`, critic to `review/pass-<n>.json`.
10. A check result (`schemas/check-result.schema.json`, written to `evidence/<T>.json`) carries `id, command, cwd, exit, verdict, stdout_tail, stderr_tail, duration_ms, ran_at, commit, covers, phase`, with `verdict` one of `pass, fail, error, skipped`. That is the fourth schema layer under a gate, which my section 2 did not name.

## Two things to check, not yet resolved

11. **Section count of the run report.** I wrote eight fixed sections, from the header comment of `cmd/report.mjs`. Explorer a94cbb2 enumerated seven headings: Ledger, Verification, Review, Phases, Agents, Failures and interventions, Orchestrator notes. Either the header counts a title block as a section or one of us miscounted. Low stakes, but someone quoting the report's shape should count them directly.
12. **The two meta-workflows are not portable.** `.claude/workflows/flightcrew-build.js` and `flightcrew-targets.js` hardcode `REPO='/Users/dylangraham/Projects/flightdeck'`. They build flightcrew itself and are not part of the run mechanism, so they sit outside the three workflow scripts I described. A hardcoded absolute path in a committed workflow is a portability defect nobody has pinned.

`flightdeck/launch/specs/flightcrew-v1/design.md:298` — "Statistical and judged checks are representable; no judge runner in v1. No verdict-sheet JSON schema for the spec judge." The missing verdict-sheet schema is a distinct gap from the missing judge runner, and my section 6 named only the runner.

Explorer aa5794d also confirms `RUNLOG.md` holds exactly three entries, all fully filled, with no literal `<fill>` present today. The placeholder mechanism is documented but currently unexercised.

# lineage-historian report — M001

Everything below is from commits, launch records and the 5-line history summaries. The single richest artefact in the whole repo is `flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md` on `flightcrew-characterization`. It carries a full human-written diagnosis for all three ended runs, and most of the mission candidates come out of its `promote:` lines.

## Seam 1 — `main`

**Attempted.** The first foray: a scaffold (`flightdeck/STRUCTURE.md`, 1c8b3fa) plus doctrine written by hand piece by piece. The manuals (8337280 "spec, rubric, testing and versioning doctrine"), the library (9f938e9 "terms, interview conventions and rubric guide"), JSON schemas for spec, plan and tests-map (7c59622), a crew manifest (6f40f82), validators and a worker renderer (a9bd004), the benches (59bf4ec), and a spec-readiness linter (efd9170). Four mission notes were written (2269973) and never acted on.

**Stalled or abandoned.** No ending document; it simply stops. Last substantive commit is 5300f0d (2026-09-03) "Add agent definitions to main". Commits b3b0a01 and 1f20b49 (2026-09-08) only touch a control-panel keep file, and 608d380 (2026-09-17) is a gitignore line. The history summary in `5ad10754_flightdeck-migration-and-branch-repair.txt` records the migration that created this state and its own loose ends: "the transferred sources still exist in dev-workspace and have not been deleted; `engage-crew` and `rubric-testing` remain based 11 commits behind `main`; `.claude/agents/` exists only on `engage-crew`, so those agents are inactive from other branches."

**Repaired or carried forward.** The schemas, the manuals and the linter all survive into the buildout. The crew roster does not: the `a9389f8c` history summary records that "main and flightcrew-buildout hold two different designs — main has no fc CLI, replaces kickoff parts with liftoff.template.yaml, and carries a rewritten crew roster."

**Unfinished intent worth a mission.**
- Four mission notes never opened: `flightdeck/missions/{extra-crew-members,flightcrew-adviser,flightcrew-library,shape-library}.md` at 2269973, present on main and on no other branch. Why it matters: these are the commander's own written intentions, orphaned by the all-in-one build that overtook them.
- `flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/liftoff.template.yaml` (4b3198a) is main's answer to the kickoff problem and exists nowhere else. Why it matters: the core spec later reinvents liftoffs as JSON recipes, so main holds a discarded design for a live question.
- `flightdeck/STRUCTURE.md` exists only on main. Why it matters: the distillation session explicitly excluded it ("build-process findings and STRUCTURE.md are excluded"), so the scaffold's own contract is undocumented on every working branch.
- Three crew roles exist only on main: `isolated-worker.md`, `spec-interviewer.md`, `worker.md`. Why it matters: the constitution names roles the buildout does not have, and main is where they went.

## Seam 2 — `engage-crew`, `constitution-research`, `rubric-testing`

**Attempted.** Three parallel probes, all in the first days of September, all trying to build the system piece by piece. `engage-crew` (forks at efd9170) built the orchestration chain role by role: 09dab8f drafted spec-builder, explorer and interface-builder; 5bb25cb added "findings, interview bundles, and judgment data for agent-spec-interviewer"; 8ec2d11 added "foundational agent definitions for orchestration chain"; 716fe4b wired validator, linter and rubric paths into spec-builder inputs. It holds a real launch folder, `flightdeck/launch/agent-spec-interviewer/`, with interview bundles, findings and an attacker run. `constitution-research` (forks at 2269973) wrote the doctrine: 8aaccd2 "foundational principles and guidelines for agentic and orchestration workflows", a06c456 "foundational entries and structure for the reference library", and 64dc36c refining orchestration principles with "adjusted fan-out order" and "guidance on outcomes, stalled states, and tool behavior". `rubric-testing` (forks at efd9170) is a measurement branch: `flightdeck/testbench/runs/` holds experiment chains for two agents across model tiers and rubric versions, namely `agent-orchestrator/experiments/{fable-v2.3, fable-4in-v2.3, opus-v2.3}` and `agent-spec-interviewer/experiments/{fable-v2.1, fable-v2.2, fable-4in}`, each a `chain.json` plus per-run verdict files, plus a `comparison.md` and an 828-line `data.json`.

**Stalled or abandoned.** None of the three has an ending document; all three simply stop. `engage-crew` and `rubric-testing` are the two the migration session named as stranded 11 commits behind main. `rubric-testing`'s single commit 194c52e is dated 2026-09-01 and is the oldest head in the repo. The commander's account says their difficulty forced the all-in-one build, and the `bab64608` history summary confirms the pivot in the user's own words: "The genesis of this was to provide some initial requirements and let the agent build out the system, rather than piece by piece over weeks."

**Repaired or carried forward.** The constitution is the one that survived and still lives. `dev/workspace/filebox/orchestration-principles.md` became the source of truth the buildout agent worked from, and it is under active review right now on `cockpit` as `orchestration-principles-review.md`. The spec-readiness rubric from the calibration work was later repaired to v2.4 and relocated (93e18c5). The `engage-crew` roles were rebuilt from scratch by the buildout (05ea8d0), not carried.

**Unfinished intent worth a mission.**
- The rubric calibration record is the only empirical evidence in the repo about agent behaviour, and the `4f6285bb` history summary reports it was poisoned: "the human grades confirming every recorded instance were the judge's own output returned, leaving the question with no verified true positive across v1 through v2.3." Why it matters: every rubric question other than QDOD.2 still rests on that same unverified record.
- `4f6285bb` also leaves open: "no agreement check has yet run against v2.4". Why it matters: the live rubric that gates every spec has never been calibrated.
- `flightdeck/launch/agent-spec-interviewer/` on `engage-crew` holds a complete spec-grown-in-its-run-folder example. Why it matters: the `a9389f8c` summary found this shape "contradicting fc launch new, which requires a pre-existing frozen spec", and the external `launch/specs/` folder was improvised around that contradiction and then documented as canonical. The contradiction is still live.
- The 64dc36c refinements on "stalled states" never reached the buildout. Why it matters: stall handling is exactly what killed characterization run 1.

## Seam 3 — `flightcrew-buildout` (+38 from main, to 2026-09-15)

**Attempted.** The all-in-one build. Per the `bab64608` summary: "an end-to-end orchestration system for flightdeck fitted into flightcrew/, launch/, testbench/ and manuals/: the fc runner and command modules, check libraries with validators and gates, ten crew roles, six hooks with a settings fragment, schemas, kickoff library and dispatch templates, three dynamic workflow scripts, manuals, and a 30-suite testbench." Method: "spec frozen first, 29 locked suites derived before implementation, contracts settled by two refutation rounds, seven Opus units behind deterministic Sonnet gates, integration to green, four sealed-room Fable critics with adjudication". Outcome: "testbench 30 of 30 suites and 527 cases with hygiene clean". It ran two launches of itself, then merged PR #1 from `flightcrew-buildout-v1` at b978b63.

**Stalled or abandoned.** Launch `flightcrew-buildout` was abandoned at verify on a wrong-check escalation: two locked suite cases "cannot be satisfied by any faithful implementation". T11 expected 2 added lines where git numstat reports 3, and the T24 import scanner read a string literal as a specifier. Launch `flightcrew-buildout-2` ended accepted-with-reservations at 19faef3. Its reservations, from RUNLOG: the tests map was revised to v2 by the orchestrator mid-build rather than after the human's log; the ending command was corrected during its own run; and five spec conflicts carried forward. Those five are B39 versus the workflow runtime (the shipped workflow scripts pass the suite but end with `export default`, which the runtime rejects), B27 worker render refusing the contracts phase the kickoff dispatches in, the I14 deny rule that would block the test-builder, the stall message on exit 0 that Claude Code does not display, and C3 one-line output versus validator lines. Six further non-blocking observations F6 through F11 are in `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout-2/report.md` and in RUNLOG, all open.

**Repaired or carried forward.** The buildout is the state every later branch tests or refactors. Its faults were then catalogued deliberately: the `a9389f8c` session wrote seven problem documents to `dev/workspace/filebox/` covering structure, testing, fc, system, kickoff, crew and endings, and `bfb87cff` distilled those eight documents and 58 recorded defects into ten spec-driving problems at `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-core/specs/interview/problems.json`.

**Unfinished intent worth a mission.**
- The central verdict of the problem hunt, from `a9389f8c`: "the builder invented a central fc runner nobody asked for that owns run state and is the human's control surface, and 57 of the spec's 78 behaviour nodes describe it." Why it matters: this is the single design fault the whole core refactor exists to undo, and it is still in place.
- `a9389f8c` also settled a target model that was never built: "freeze is enforced from the document's own status field and phase state lives in launch.json as an index a web page writes; a runner survives only as leaf commands behind pages and hooks". Why it matters: a designed-but-unbuilt architecture with a web page as the human's control surface.
- The five carried spec conflicts in the buildout-2 reservation line, above all B39. Why it matters: the workflow scripts ship green and do not run, which is why both later runs had to be conducted by hand.
- Commit 1c81888 added `spec-altitude.md` and refined `spec-description.md`, the last commit on the branch, after every run had ended. Why it matters: it is doctrine written from the core interview's altitude lesson and it has never been exercised by a run.
- Commit 8a56624 added the orchestrator-pattern source guides and 5b2d6ba deleted them nine commits later; `library/source/orchestrator-pattern/` persists on later branches. Why it matters: the sources the builder worked from are the arbiter of every divergence, and their location has moved twice.
- Two roles the constitution names are absent, and "dispatch is sealed for three roles and freehand for four" (`a9389f8c`). Why it matters: the crew is inconsistent by design, and run 2 later pinned four crew defects confirming it.

## Seam 4 — `flightcrew-core` (+37, to 2026-09-13)

**Attempted.** The second attempt at the system, driven by the ten distilled problems. Per `9b679556`: "Settled: launch/<name>/{specs/,runs/run-<n>/} layout with launch.json as an index and no phase or gate state; configuration under flightdeck/.controlcenter/; freeze in the document; liftoffs as JSON recipes driving a workflow suite; a steward role runs scripts for workflows; a stalled worker gets one stronger attempt then the run stops; the ending after the report is the human's; the flight dispatcher is deferred". Nine judge passes and one attack, 44 findings absorbed. The spec at `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-core/specs/spec.v1.json` holds 8 scope, 8 constraints, 12 interfaces, 35 behaviours, 14 edges and 9 decisions. Its SC1 is blunt: "the superseded v1 runner, hooks, kickoff library, dispatch templates, schemas ... and MANIFEST.txt are deleted."

**Stalled or abandoned.** Held, exactly as the commander says, and the artefacts say why in two places. The spec's own `status` field reads `"draft"`, not frozen. It was frozen at 852b77c, then ef44303 reopened it after the outcome-altitude rewrite, so as `9b679556` puts it: "the draft was frozen at 852b77c then reopened as draft after the altitude rewrite, so the freeze commit on the branch is void." The `launch.json` still points `current_run: 1` at `base_commit` 7c5913e, the void freeze, with `tests_map: null` and `lock_commit: null`. The same summary states the decision to wait for a suite: "open a separate characterization launch whose product is a test suite at the human-observable boundary (labelled kept-deliberately or may-change), run it under the v1 tooling as the first real run, and cut flightcrew-core to the deltas (fc ripped out, workflows as conductor, launch/run layout, liftoffs, added roles, three verdicts) against that suite." No repair commit exists. An earlier v2 draft was abandoned outright before this v1 was written.

**Repaired or carried forward.** The rubric repair (93e18c5, QDOD.2 to v2.4, moved to `flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/rubrics/`) landed here and is shared with buildout. The ten-problem register is the branch's durable asset.

**Unfinished intent worth a mission.**
- The whole repair. Per SC1 and the `9b679556` delta list: rip out fc, make workflows the conductor, adopt the launch/run layout, add liftoffs, add the missing roles, implement three verdict kinds. Why it matters: this is the epic the entire repo is arranged around, and it has not started.
- Two spec contradictions await a human, from `3b316953`: "I8 (reused return schemas set additionalProperties false yet every return must carry target and fix) and I13 (check-result keeps a phase field that D5 abolishes)". Why it matters: the spec cannot be re-frozen until they are resolved, so they block the epic.
- Two nodes are unverified as unprovable: "B70 and E18 went to unverified as unprovable before a probe harness exists". Why it matters: a probe harness for agent behaviour is a mission in its own right.
- `flightdeck/.controlcenter/` is specified in SC2 and exists nowhere as working configuration. Why it matters: it is the seam the later web control surface plugs into.
- `9b679556` records the interview method lessons: "never invent mechanisms (run.json, fc-end, HALT.json, gates were all invented and removed); answer questions before acting; behaviours are outcomes with their reason, not dispatch mechanics". Why it matters: these are the guardrails the buildout lacked, and they belong in doctrine rather than a transcript.

**The void run.** Run 1 was opened at 7c5913e and the test-builder ran against the then-frozen spec. `3b316953` records the output: "a draft tests map at flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-core/specs/tests-map.v1.json with 51 checks and total coverage, baselined at commit 7c5913e with 48 fails, 3 world-state passes". The reopen voided the freeze under it. Commit 27f6969 is the commander committing that orphaned output rather than losing it: 97 files, 8158 insertions, comprising 49 suites, per-check wrappers at `runs/run-1/checks/T*.mjs`, four new testbench libraries (`core-lib`, `workflow-stub`, `workflow-scan`, `orchestrator-rules`) and two locked fixtures, `sample-launch-core` in the target layout and `sample-transcript` holding obedient, control and pull-request-failure orchestrator sessions. The map's own `status` is `"draft"`.

## Seam 5 — `flightcrew-characterization` and children

**Attempted.** A complete test suite over flightcrew as it stands, so the core refactor has something to lean on. The frozen spec's intent is explicit: "Flightcrew's test suite checks only its runner's commands. Nothing exercises a role, a hook or a workflow the way a working session does ... Flightcrew is about to be refactored, and without a suite that describes it whole, every part the refactor touches is changed blind." SC4 forbids repair: a defect found is pinned, not fixed. SC5 forbids reading flightcrew-core at all. Spec frozen at eca2bba, tests map frozen at 266b10f with 61 checks. Two runs followed.

**Stalled or abandoned.** Run 1 was abandoned at implement (36f1536), and its RUNLOG entry is the most valuable single document found in this survey. Symptom: "U0 (contracts, second dispatch) spent its 25-turn budget ... and stopped without delivering a worker return". Cause, stated flatly: "tooling. Ten faults, none in the spec or the map". The ten: no tool invokes a workflow, so the workflow shape is unusable; fc worker render refuses a contracts unit in phase contracts; the worktree guard refuses the fc shim, fixed mid-run at 85ed27e; three turn numbers exist and only frontmatter maxTurns binds, stopping U0 with no return; a stopped agent cannot be resumed and a fresh dispatch fails on the existing unit branch; explorers count against the agents ceiling; fc budget counts gate and human waits as run minutes, 1306 of them recorded; no command changes a ceiling; escalation.json holds only the newest escalation; fc return has no `--stdin` and the orchestrator holds no Write. Plus two more: "fc check --baseline rewrote the frozen map's baseline commit, and fc launch pin's own instruction to allow the check commands put settings.json outside the boundary after the lock."

**Repaired or carried forward.** Run 2 was fixed "by not using it": conducted by a custom workflow written for the run, checks invoked by path, no active launch and so no hooks, spec and map unchanged. It worked. The report at `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-characterization/runs/run-2/report.md` (commit 4fd81d8, on `run/flightcrew-characterization-2`) records 58 of 61 checks green at 4046d1e, `run-all.mjs` exit 0 with 33 suites, critic verdict pass with no blocking finding, and six units U0 through U5 with only U2 refuted.

**What the suite characterizes.** Every part of flightcrew as it stands at the run's base: all 35 subcommands, the six hooks, the templates and their slots, the crew role files, the workflow scripts and their distributed `.claude/` copies, the schemas, validators and gates, plus output protocol, hygiene and continuity self-tests. The three red checks are all check concerns, none fixable inside the allowed paths. T44 sweep-templates-1 is red because `constitution-fragment.md` has no slot, key or version comment, so no alteration can turn a case red and the human must choose between an exemption and a different alteration. T32 spec-copies is red at the base before any unit ran, because the three spec copies diverge from the frozen spec and `check-lib.mjs` hard-codes `SPEC_COMMIT ee8c088` and `FREEZE_COMMIT eca2bba`. T37 scope-untouched is also red at the base, because the run-2 setup commits changed four crew files, `worker-dispatch.template.md`, the `.claude/agents/flightcrew/` copies and `.claude/settings.json`.

The six pinned defects: every role except four carries maxTurns but explorer, verifier and critic carry none; the roster turns 12, 25, 15 and 20 do not match the files, with implementer at 200; spec-judge and spec-attacker carry no inputs line; the spec-builder body exceeds the 60-line limit; `fc launch gate G3 approve` records a gate decision on a launch that has already ended; and `validate-tests-map.mjs` judges coverage against `--spec` although its own header says the spec beside the map is read first. Commit 3043f6c reverted a check because U2's second repair "pinned the heading against a requirement no manual states", which is the suite refusing to invent its own standard, the rule working as intended.

**Unfinished intent worth a mission.**
- The run-2 result is stranded. `run/flightcrew-characterization-2` is 16 commits ahead of `flightcrew-characterization` and was never merged. Why it matters: the suite the core repair is waiting on exists and is not on the branch anyone would look at.
- The RUNLOG has no run-2 entry: "The run-log entry in `flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md` is not stubbed; no `fc launch end` ran in a runner-free run." Why it matters: the commander's own rule is that a run is not opened until the previous entry carries no `<fill>` line, so the ledger is blocking.
- No scenario set was built: "the statistical and judged checks pass empty" under D3, with T16 through T21 all green-because-empty. Why it matters: six of the seven check classes the spec set out to use are untested, and the judged and statistical classes are exactly what agent-shaped work needs.
- The reviewer's three acts on a fresh checkout and the transcript reading were never done. Why it matters: the run's acceptance is incomplete by its own definition.
- Four critic observations left open, F1 through F4, including F3 where "a regression from writing to logging a failure stays green" and F4 where a case "claims a source it does not have". Why it matters: two of them are holes in the suite's own rigour.
- Commit 95a8cae on `flightcrew-characterization-1/contracts` is 31 files of protocol fixes inside U0's paths, checks unverified, "held for the human to salvage or delete". Why it matters: it is the only piece of run 1 with any value and it has an explicit pending decision on it.
- The RUNLOG `promote:` line names what must reach the core spec: "faults 1 to 10 above, above all that a conducting role must be able to invoke a workflow, that the harness turn limit is the only real budget and must leave a turn for the return, that waiting on a human is not run time, and that a frozen document must not be rewritten by a tool." Why it matters: this is a written, prioritised input to the core spec that has not been absorbed.

## Reconciliation

**Buildout's two own commits past 1b0a8ba** are 56bfb87 (2026-09-13) "Move the flightcrew-core launch to its own branch" and 1c81888 (2026-09-15) "Add spec-altitude.md and refine spec-description.md". The first is bookkeeping, the second is doctrine written after the core interview taught the altitude lesson.

**Core's one own commit past 1b0a8ba** is 27f6969 (2026-09-13) "Commit the test-builder output of the void flightcrew-core run", described in the void-run paragraph above. Core carries no repair work at all.

**The characterization suite tests the buildout state and carries no core changes.** The merge-base of `flightcrew-core` and `flightcrew-characterization` is 1b0a8ba, so 27f6969 is not in it. Characterization forks from buildout at 56bfb87 and therefore also lacks 1c81888. Spec SC5 says so explicitly: "the flightcrew-core spec, its branch, its layout, workflows, liftoffs and roles are not read and not tested." One consequence to flag: core's 27f6969 added 49 suites under `flightdeck/testbench/suites/` and run 2 built its own 33-suite tree in the same directory, from a base that has never seen them. Landing both is a merge conflict in a shared namespace, not a fast-forward.

**Pure ancestors, safe to forget as branches.** `flightcrew-buildout-v1` (ancestor of buildout, merged as PR #1 at b978b63); `run/flightcrew-core-1` (ancestor of `flightcrew-core`); `run/flightcrew-characterization-1` (ancestor of `flightcrew-characterization`); and all six `worktree-wf_*` branches plus `flightcrew-characterization-2/contracts` and `flightcrew-characterization-2/roles-workflows-distributed`, every one of which is confirmed contained in `run/flightcrew-characterization-2`.

**Not debris, keep.** `flightcrew-characterization-1/contracts` is one commit past its merge-base and is the salvage-or-delete decision named in RUNLOG. `run/flightcrew-characterization-2` is 16 commits of unmerged finished work and is the most valuable unlanded ref in the repo.

**Stale remotes.** `origin/flightcrew-buildout` sits at 93e18c5, eleven commits behind local. `origin/flightcrew-characterization` sits at 56ca431, missing run 2 entirely. `origin/main` is two behind local. There is also a local branch literally named `origin` at 1f20b49, which is debris from the migration.

## Candidate epic missions

1. **Land the characterization suite.** Merge `run/flightcrew-characterization-2` into `flightcrew-characterization`, write the run-2 RUNLOG entry, resolve T32 and T37 (both red at base from setup commits), and decide T44. Evidence: `runs/run-2/report.md` at 4fd81d8; the ledger rule in `flightdeck/launch/FLIGHTLOG.md`. Seam 5. Unblocks everything else.
2. **Absorb the two run diagnoses into the core spec and re-freeze it.** The RUNLOG `promote:` lines from both ended runs, plus resolving I8 and I13, plus accepting or rejecting B70 and E18. Evidence: RUNLOG on `flightcrew-characterization`; the `3b316953` history summary; spec `status: "draft"` at `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-core/specs/spec.v1.json`. Seam 4. The gate on the whole refactor.
3. **Execute the core repair against the landed suite.** Rip out fc, make workflows the conductor, adopt launch/run layout, liftoffs, missing roles, three verdicts. Evidence: SC1 of the core spec; the delta list in `9b679556`. Seams 3 and 4. The epic the repo exists for.
4. **Make a conducting role able to invoke a workflow.** Named as the first of ten faults and singled out in `promote:` as the one that matters most. Evidence: RUNLOG run-1 cause line; B39 `export default` in the buildout-2 reservations. Seam 5 into seam 3. Without it every run is conducted by hand.
5. **Fix the budget and turn model.** One real limit against three recorded numbers, no turn reserved for the return, human waits counted as run minutes, no command changes a ceiling, a stopped agent unresumable. Evidence: RUNLOG faults 4 through 8. Seam 5. This is what actually killed run 1.
6. **Build the scenario and probe harness.** D3 left T16 through T21 passing empty, and core's B70 and E18 are unprovable without a probe harness. Evidence: run-2 report "Not done" section; `3b316953`. Seams 4 and 5. Judged and statistical checks are the only way to test agent-shaped behaviour.
7. **Reconcile the two testbench trees.** Core's 49 orphaned suites from 27f6969 against run 2's 33 live suites in the same directory, built from a base that never saw them. Evidence: `git show --stat 27f6969`; the run-2 suite list. Seams 4 and 5. A silent collision waiting at merge time.
8. **Recalibrate the spec rubric.** No verified true positive across v1 to v2.3, the calibration record contaminated by the judge's own output, and no agreement check yet run against live v2.4. Evidence: the `4f6285bb` history summary; `flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/rubrics/spec/`. Seam 2 into seam 3. Every spec is gated by an uncalibrated instrument.

## Harness claims to verify

These are things the artefacts assert about Claude Code itself. None were tested. All are quoted from the records, and each should be checked against current documentation before a mission leans on it.
- Only the agent frontmatter `maxTurns` actually limits an agent; other recorded turn numbers do not bind. RUNLOG run-1 fault 4.
- An agent stopped by the turn limit cannot be resumed, and a fresh dispatch fails when the unit branch already exists. RUNLOG run-1 fault 5.
- A stall message is not displayed by Claude Code on exit 0. Buildout-2 reservation line.
- The workflow runtime rejects a script ending in `export default`. Spec B39, buildout-2 reservations.
- The orchestrator role holds no Write tool, which is why `fc return` needing `--stdin` was fatal. RUNLOG run-1 fault 10.
- The worktree isolation guard refuses a shell shim, requiring `node fc.mjs` instead of `fc`. RUNLOG run-1 fault 3, fixed mid-run at 85ed27e.
- Explorer subagents count against a run's agent ceiling. RUNLOG run-1 fault 6.
- Whether run 2's "every agent ran on opus" and the crew's `model: fable` settings still name valid model tiers. Run-2 report and migration summary `5ad10754`.

# lineage-historian report — M001 (revised)

Revision note: this supersedes my first report. Five explorer reports were recovered from disk and reconciled in. They added one major artefact I had missed, corrected two of my numbers, and one of their own claims was wrong and is corrected here. Every changed fact was re-verified against git before inclusion. Changes are marked **[new]** or **[corrected]**.

The two most important documents in the repo are `dev/workspace/plans/flightcrew-features.md` on `flightcrew-buildout`, which is a written ten-feature roadmap split into three launches, and `flightdeck/launch/RUNLOG.md` on `flightcrew-characterization`, which carries a human-written diagnosis of all three ended runs. Between them they already contain most of the epic missions, written by the commander.

## Seam 1 — `main`

**Attempted.** The first foray: a scaffold (`flightdeck/STRUCTURE.md`, 1c8b3fa) plus doctrine written by hand piece by piece. The manuals (8337280 "spec, rubric, testing and versioning doctrine"), the library (9f938e9 "terms, interview conventions and rubric guide"), JSON schemas for spec, plan and tests-map (7c59622), a crew manifest (6f40f82), validators and a worker renderer (a9bd004), the benches (59bf4ec), and a spec-readiness linter (efd9170). Four mission notes were written (2269973) and never acted on.

**Stalled or abandoned.** No ending document; it simply stops. Last substantive commit is 5300f0d (2026-09-03) "Add agent definitions to main". Commits b3b0a01 and 1f20b49 (2026-09-08) only touch a control-panel keep file, and 608d380 (2026-09-17) is a gitignore line. The history summary `5ad10754_flightdeck-migration-and-branch-repair.txt` records the migration that created this state and its own loose ends: "the transferred sources still exist in dev-workspace and have not been deleted; `engage-crew` and `rubric-testing` remain based 11 commits behind `main`; `.claude/agents/` exists only on `engage-crew`, so those agents are inactive from other branches."

**Repaired or carried forward.** The schemas, manuals and linter survive into the buildout. The crew roster does not: the `a9389f8c` summary records that "main and flightcrew-buildout hold two different designs — main has no fc CLI, replaces kickoff parts with liftoff.template.yaml, and carries a rewritten crew roster."

**Unfinished intent worth a mission.**
- Four mission notes never opened: `flightdeck/missions/{extra-crew-members,flightcrew-adviser,flightcrew-library,shape-library}.md` at 2269973, on main and no other branch. Why it matters: the commander's own written intentions, orphaned by the all-in-one build that overtook them.
- **[new]** The port of the crew to main at 5300f0d is one day stale. I verified with `diff <(git show engage-crew:.claude/agents/spec-builder.md) <(git show main:.claude/agents/spec-builder.md)`: main's copy still names the run folder `dev/workspace/runs/<spec-name>/` and omits the validator, linter and rubric paths that engage-crew's final commit 716fe4b added. - `flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/liftoff.template.yaml` (4b3198a) is main's answer to the kickoff problem and exists nowhere else. Why it matters: the core spec later reinvents liftoffs as JSON recipes, so main holds a discarded design for a live question.
